LC76 OVERLAND REFERENCE LIBRARY — SESSION HISTORY
================================================================================
Created: 27 July 2026 (nineteenth session), split out of
LC76_Project_Instructions.txt.

WHAT THIS IS. A RECORD, not an instruction. It holds the session-by-session
narrative blocks (sixteenth session, 24 Jul 2026, and earlier) that formerly
lived in the Project Instructions header. Blocks are verbatim and newest-first.

AUTHORITY. LC76_Project_Instructions.txt is authoritative for all doctrine and
working conventions. WHERE THIS FILE AND THE PROJECT INSTRUCTIONS DISAGREE, THE
PROJECT INSTRUCTIONS WIN. Nothing here is a live rule; every load-bearing rule
was promoted into the Project Instructions body before these blocks were moved
(see the promotion ledger, LC76_PI_Doctrine_Promotion_Ledger.txt).

EXTENDED 29 July 2026 (twenty-third session). Four further blocks — the
seventeenth, eighteenth, nineteenth and twentieth sessions — were moved here
verbatim, restoring the Project Instructions header to the "current + two
priors in full" convention. The same safety order was held: the four blocks
were swept for doctrine BEFORE the move and SIX narrative-only live rules were
promoted into the Project Instructions body first (archives-not-reconstructed
with its redaction-notice and labelled-gap corollaries; the precedence rule
between these two files; the Class P cover/medical retain ruling; the hard-404
working-method blocker; and the three-layer "site looks old" triage). The
sweep also found a DIAGNOSIS that had never been converted into an open item
— the Pages/PWA layer question from the nineteenth session, whose reserved
number was consumed elsewhere — which was logged as OI-57 rather than
archived into silence.

EXTENDED 31 July 2026 (integrity check). Four further blocks — the
twenty-second, twenty-third, twenty-fourth and twenty-fifth sessions — were
moved here verbatim from the Project Instructions header. On 31 Jul these
four were compressed to one-line entries in the PI archived-sessions list,
but the matching move to this file was omitted at the time; this restores
it. The blocks are reproduced exactly as they stood in the PI at commit
12ca17fc (30 Jul 2026); no narrative was altered, removed or reconstructed,
and the PI's one-line summaries for these sessions remain in place.

EXTENDED 24 September 2026 (PI restructure, Session A — sixty-second
session; LC76_PI_Restructure_Spec.txt, approved 24 Sep 2026). Thirty-three
further blocks — the fifty-eighth session back to the twenty-sixth — were
moved here verbatim from the Project Instructions header, restoring the
"current + two priors" convention (now a standing rule in the PI). The same
safety order was held: the doctrine sweep ran FIRST (spec §6) and rows P1–P7
were promoted into the PI body in the same session before any block moved;
the four open obligations found only in narrative (spec §7 O1–O4) were
captured in the Open Items Register as OI-110, OI-111 and OI-112 before the
move. Also moved verbatim, as a labelled section at the END of this file: the
PI body's CLOSED WORKSTREAMS span. Each block was sha256-verified at its
destination. NO narrative was altered, removed or reconstructed. The PI's
one-line archive index now lives below, extended with one line per newly
moved session, built mechanically from each block's own date, ordinal and
capitalised title phrase — no new summarising.

EXTENDED 24 September 2026 (PI restructure, Session B — sixty-third
session). Two further moves, both verbatim. (1) The fifty-ninth session block
was moved here from the Project Instructions header under the header-
housekeeping rule (current + two priors), with one index line built
mechanically from its own date, ordinal and capitalised title phrase. (2) The
Project Instructions body was condensed to the spec §2 structure; the ORIGINAL
WORDING of every unit that was rewritten, merged, or replaced by a pointer —
including every incident story — was moved as a labelled section at the END of
this file ("PI BODY TEXT MOVED IN RESTRUCTURE SESSION B"), one sub-heading per
unit, each carrying its sha256 and verified present exactly once. NO narrative
was altered, removed or reconstructed. These texts are a RECORD, not live
rules: the condensed rule in the Project Instructions governs.

▌REDACTION NOTICE — 28 July 2026
This file was REDACTED on 28 Jul 2026 under the RC1 / name reduction pass.
Registration-certificate VALUES (register number, series name as registered,
vehicle description, first-licensed date, previous licence number,
certificate issue date and office), the EXACT LICENCE-CARD AND DISC
EXPIRY DATES (coarsened to month/year in place), and FULL LEGAL NAMES
were withdrawn and
replaced with pointers to PRIVATE ANNEX §1/§2, held offline and never
committed. NO narrative was altered, removed or reconstructed — only values
were withdrawn. Where a pointer now stands, the fact WAS recorded here at the
time and remains recoverable from the annex. The repo history was NOT
rewritten (owner ruling, 28 Jul 2026), so prior commits still carry the
original values; this redaction is forward-looking only.

EXTENDED — 28 Jul 2026, THIRD TRANCHE (Karoo date-exposure pass). The
KAROO TRIP DATES were withdrawn to PRIVATE ANNEX §4 and generalised in
place: the itinerary now runs on RELATIVE DAY NUMBERS, and references in
this file are coarsened to "winter 2026", "on return" or "before
departure". The finding was the same aggregation problem — a named crew,
a distinctive vehicle, a known region and a precisely dated multi-week
absence from a South African property, all public. Again NO narrative was
altered, removed or reconstructed; only date VALUES were withdrawn.
UNLIKE §1/§2/§3 THIS SUPPRESSION IS TEMPORARY: dates are dangerous before
the fact and harmless after it, so annex §4 carries a RESTORE-AFTER note
and the values return at the R23 §7 post-trip review.

Every real-world change that retires a term is ONE old->new row in the log, and ADDING that row is what arms the gate — so a NEW change cannot slip past without arming the check [answers the owner's objection that a term-list fails the moment something new changes: the list is DATA in the log, and the row is the same line you write to record the change]. HONEST LIMIT [in the log header]: it catches SUPERSESSIONS that go through the log, not fresh fabrications. Seeded with 11 supersessions, each with exempt_files / exempt_line so historical ledgers, comparison tables and correction notes are not false-flagged. FIRST RUN earned its place [16 hits: 2 false positives tuned out — a .racor-card CSS class, RA-1 matching RA-10 — the rest all tracked]; it even CAUGHT ITS OWN AUTHOR — a changelog note I wrote quoted the retired '265/70 carried, 265/75 planned' wording, and the gate flagged it, enforcing the 28-Jul 'a correction note must not embed the superseded token' rule automatically. PRIOR (7 August 2026, forty-sixth session (DATA-PLATE FACTS CAPTURE. The manufacturer's build/data plate (VIN, model code HZJ76R-RKMRS, weights, C/TR, A/TM, PLANT) was photographed by the owner and its figures reconciled into the library. CONFIRMS three existing values with physical E1 evidence — GVM 3,000 kg (was Toyota-confirmed Jul 2026, now plate-verified), the chassis/model code, and the R151F transmission. THE OPERATIONAL POINT — the axle maxima sum to 3,660 kg, so GVM is not the only ceiling: on a rear-heavy expedition load the REAR 2,180 kg limit is the likely binding constraint and can be exceeded while the vehicle is still legal on the 3,000 kg GVM. RA-1 (OI-1) is therefore reframed as a PER-AXLE weighbridge with the rear axle the number to watch — annotated in the OIR. MODEL YEAR: the plate carries NO model-year field, so it cannot confirm 2009 — OI-31 stays OPEN with the plate route recorded as exhausted (year rests on the marketing descriptor alone). Archived PI/Session blocks naming the old §13 LEFT INTACT (archives not reconstructed). (2) MOBILE WIDE-TABLE OVERFLOW. Root cause: the mobile rule made .spec-table display:block+overflow-x:auto but with NO min-width, so wide tables shrank to fit and squashed instead of scrolling; a bare min-width on that display:block table would have OVERFLOWED the body (overflow-x:clip) and clipped, so the fix is a .table-scroll WRAPPER (overflow-x:auto + overscroll-behavior-x:contain, matching the v1.7 PWA pattern) around each wide table, with a MOBILE-ONLY min-width on the inner table. Widths TUNED PER TABLE BY CONTENT DENSITY (a flat by-column scheme was wrong): classes mwn 420 / mw3 380 / mw4 480 / mw5 540 / mw6 640 / mw7 720. Final assignment — §5 load-breakdown mw7 (text-heavy); §7 charging mw6; §8 switch mw5; §8 scenario mw6; §10 SA-challenges mw6; §11 'daily drive' table mwn (deliberately NARROW: its cells are percentages/ticks, the first-pass mw6 made it read oversized); §12 symbols mw6. EIGHT wrapped tables total (the §11 question-③ table wrapped in a later pass). 2-col tables left unwrapped (comfortable). Print unaffected: min-width is mobile-scoped and the wrapper is forced overflow:visible in @media print. (3) §12 SYMBOLS col-1 nowrap (.symtable td:first-child) so multi-char symbols (A (or I), SoC %, C rate) hold one line instead of stacking. (4) STATS ALIGNMENT — the .stats grid was 2-col with align-items:start on mobile, so five uneven cards went ragged; set to ONE column on mobile, fixing BOTH the §12 'five most important numbers' block and the §3 SoC-voltage block (same component). (5) §11 TEXT CLIPPING — the wild-camp question blocks are grid items AND flex containers; only the inner flex child could shrink, so the block ran past the screen and body overflow-x:clip cut the text. Fixed with min-width:0 on BOTH .do-item (grid item) and .do-item>div (flex child, + max-width:100%), and the loose question-③ table wrapped in .table-scroll (mwn) so it scrolls rather than pushing text off-screen. (6) §8 SWITCH-PANEL Position column — a rowspan on the Switch column meant nth-child could not target Position, so the three Position cells (TOP/MIDDLE/BOTTOM) were tagged .pos with .spec-table td.pos{min-width:150px} so each reads in ~2 lines. (7) §4/§6 FONT CONSISTENCY — the component-card .row-value was set in IBM Plex Mono while all other explanatory prose is Oswald (--sans), so full-sentence explanations ('What it does', 'BMS job', …) rendered monospace and looked different; .row-value switched to --sans. PRIOR (5 August 2026, forty-first session) (COLD-CHARGE EXPLANATORY FIX
across R5/R7/R26, and OI-99 OPENED — the Explanatory-depth "gems" sweep. This is
the first concrete instance of the pattern the owner spotted: facts recorded
without the mechanism that makes them usable. THE FIX: three R5 sites stated the
sub-zero BMS charge cutoff as a MANUAL instruction ("verify the cutoff is set
correctly before cold trips", "simply avoid charging below 0°C", "avoid charging
until the battery is above 0°C") — which misleads, because the cutoff is intrinsic
cell protection: automatic, not a user-configurable setting, and impossible to
bypass from the switch panel. All three rewritten to say the BMS blocks charge
automatically and releases on its own as the cells warm; discharge is fine below
0°C, only charging is held; and the ONLY human element is planning for a delayed
alternator recharge on frosty highland mornings (no low-temp heating pad is
fitted, so the house bank will not recharge until it warms an hour or two into the
drive). OWNERSHIP-FIRST SWEEP of R7/R26 per owner instruction: R7 already carried
the correct framing ("the BMS handles this automatically, no user action needed")
and was left untouched; R26's "do not force-charge a cold battery" line lightly
sharpened to reinforce that there is nothing to force and no cutoff to set. R7
needed no edit; R5 + R26 edited. OI-99 OPENED (Section B, WAITING-DATE post-Karoo):
the Explanatory-depth sweep itself, chartered so the broader hunt is not forgotten
— run electrical-first as a pilot (R5/R7/R26/R32 + calculators), then charter the
library-wide pass off the pilot; the RA-8 / Mary-Ann dependence is the named why;
the meta-lesson (build on vehicle-confirmed facts, not inferred ones) is recorded
on the item; it should precede EX-11 so the laminated field pack carries the
explained versions. COUNT RECONCILIATION (roll-forward reconciliation caught this):

PRIOR (5 August 2026, fortieth session) (R5 §13 SWITCH-POSITION CORRECTION.
The §13 Daily-Operations switch table and scenario table shipped in the thirty-ninth
session carried the three MultiPlus positions in the WRONG ORDER — they were inferred
from the generic Victron on/off/charge-only layout rather than confirmed against this
install. Owner confirmed the actual wiring with Manjaro Industries (5 Aug 2026), with
a written explainer of all three states. CORRECTED positions: TOP = Inverter & Charger
(full hybrid — charges from shore AND inverts to hold the sockets live if shore drops,
<20 ms transfer); MIDDLE = OFF (everything shut down, sockets dead even on shore, the
transfer relay stays open, minimal standby draw); BOTTOM = Charger Only (charges from
shore and passes shore AC to the sockets, but the inverter is disabled — if shore
drops the sockets go dead and the battery is never drained). KEY POINT recorded on the
document: OFF is the MIDDLE position, not the bottom — unusual, install-specific, and
the exact trap the inference fell into. The campsite/overnight default is BOTTOM
(charger only): charges + live sockets, but no battery-drain risk on a shore cut; TOP
is reserved for when uninterrupted 230V is genuinely needed. Both §13 tables corrected;
the "why three positions" box rewritten to explain the bottom-vs-top choice by what
happens when shore FAILS; a provenance line added ("confirmed with Manjaro Industries,
5 Aug 2026 — do not correct to the generic Victron order") so a future pass does not
re-introduce the error. NO NEW OI — this is a same-programme correction to the thirty-
ninth session's own output; open count unchanged at 67, next free OI-99. CACHE STAYS
v26 — content-only. Re-pinned to HEAD d30a0f87 (Session 2 committed byte-identical).

in BLACK AND WHITE — faint text barely showing. Diagnosed by measurement, not

labels fall to grey L≈157 on an L≈237 ground (Δ80, the faint text), and the
fuel-label is rgba(...,0.6) on a dark fill that VANISHES if "print background
graphics" is off. Two failure modes: muted colours too light in mono, and
light-on-dark text depending on backgrounds printing. This is a SEPARATE AXIS
from OI-60 (which is print FONT-SIZE, rem-vs-root) — recorded as its own item,

PRIOR (29 July 2026, twenty-fifth session) (THE REGISTER SHAPE PASS RAN
AND THEN THE SESSION TURNED INTO SOMETHING LARGER: AN ASSURANCE CHARTER OVER
THE OPEN ITEMS REGISTER — "AUDIT OF TRUTH" — APPROVED AS THE BASIS FOR A
SEVEN-SESSION PROGRAMME; THE DASHBOARD WAS BUILT AND THEN DELIBERATELY LEFT
UNWIRED; A MASTERSHIP MAP RULED; CACHE STAYS v25; next free OI-68).

WHAT HAPPENED, AND THE SEQUENCING ERROR THAT CAUSED IT. The owner asked for a
"register shape pass then dashboard". SHAPE WAS READ AS FORMATTING — line
positions, status on its own line — because that was the reading that let the
build start. The register did not need tidier layout. It needed its ENTRIES
checked: is this one job or three, is the source real, does the status mean
anything. The dashboard was built on unchecked entries, and every defect
underneath surfaced as a complaint about the page. GENERAL LESSON: WHEN AN
INSTRUCTION HAS A CHEAP READING AND AN EXPENSIVE ONE, THE CHEAP READING IS
SUSPECT — especially when it is the one that lets work begin immediately.

THE OWNER'S FIVE TRANSACTION ASSERTIONS FOUND A HOLE IN THE METHODOLOGY IN ONE
MESSAGE. Issue 1 of the charter carried seven assertions: COMPLETENESS was
covered, OCCURRENCE obliquely, CLASSIFICATION only as a remediation construct.
ACCURACY AND CUT-OFF WERE ABSENT ALTOGETHER — in a register whose entries turn
on mileages, intervals and dates. The audit would have certified entries whose
figures nobody had tested, and looked rigorous doing it. Ten assertions now.

AND THE ACCURACY ASSERTION IMMEDIATELY EARNED ITS PLACE. Tested against the
service ledger: OI-62 states the coolant flush is ~86,006 km overdue, while the
ledger carries coolant work 2,752 km ago; OI-63 states the bearing repack is
~63,684 km overdue while the replacement it cites was 39,800 km ago. NEITHER IS
ASSERTED WRONG — a top-up is not a flush, an interval may run from an earlier
event. The finding is that NOBODY CAN CURRENTLY TELL, and both figures justify
workshop spend.

THE ITEM LIFECYCLE IS THE OWNER'S CONTRIBUTION AND IT RESTRUCTURED THE CHARTER.
Issue 1 audited the balance — the 40 items as they stand — and never asked how
an item is BORN. Completeness cannot be established by tracing between files: if
something never enters the system, two instruments agree perfectly and both are
wrong. Five origination streams are now defined, with their capture points; the
fifth — SYSTEMIC GENERATION — is the one that produces items nobody thought to
raise, and eight generators are chartered at charter Appendix A.

MASTERSHIP RULED (see WORKING CONVENTIONS). Three instruments were each behaving
as master, which is why the working papers still carry RA-4 as owner-gated five
days after the ruling that closed it.

THE REGISTER STAYS A .txt AND THAT WAS THE RIGHT CALL. A spreadsheet master was
proposed and rejected on the owner's reasoning: a .xlsx is a binary blob, cannot
be diffed, and this whole working method rests on SHA-pinning and reading what
changed. The field-discipline objection died the same day — a parser read all 40
entries into structured data with full fidelity. A TEXT FILE WITH AN ENFORCED
SCHEMA IS A DATABASE, and it is also readable. THE CONDITION IS NOT OPTIONAL: a
schema is only real if something rejects violations. A shape convention was
written in the morning and five entries still had to be repaired in the
afternoon.

THE DASHBOARD EXISTS AND IS DELIBERATELY UNWIRED (owner ruling). It is on GitHub,
reachable by direct URL, NOT linked from index.html, NOT precached, NOT in sw.js
— because it displays a register whose entries have not been audited, and wiring
it would present unverified data as the library's answer to "what is open".
Wiring is a Session 6 action and carries the CACHE bump v25 → v26.

FOUR ERRORS THIS SESSION, ALL DISCLOSED. (1) The sequencing error above.
(2) A closed-log count of 60 written into the register before checking — the
real figure is 61, one entry starting with a bracket that the first probe
skipped. (3) Detail text rendered with a hard line break at every register line,
producing a narrow ragged column; the owner reported it as a layout fault and it
was a data-handling fault. (4) THE INSTRUCTIVE ONE: the charter asserted that
the manual-prescribed origination stream had no capture point, on the evidence
of an empty Schedule sheet in the service workbook. IT WAS WRONG — LC76_Service_
Checklist.html carries 66 items, 64 with intervals, every one provenance-tagged.
The error was made by asserting a gap from ONE instrument without establishing
the population: the exact failure the charter exists to address, committed inside
the charter itself. The empty sheet is now a housekeeping finding (F-K).

FILES THIS ROUND: 3 pushed — LC76_Open_Items_Register.txt (shape pass, meta-line
repairs, 40 plain-language WHAT lines), LC76_Open_Items_Dashboard.html (prototype,
unwired), LC76_Audit_of_Truth_Charter.txt (new governance file) — plus this file
and LC76_Library_Register.txt. Dashboard validation clean: depth walks 0/0/0 on
nine tag types, ids unique, TOC contract 5/5 in order, node --check clean, 40
items rendered, no browser storage. NO LIBRARY FILE WAS ADDED TO THE PRECACHE —
CACHE STAYS v25.

PRIOR (29 July 2026, twenty-fourth session) (THE LIBRARY'S FIRST USER
GUIDE BUILT — LC76_How_To_Use_This_Library.html, INFRASTRUCTURE CLASS, NINE
SECTIONS; A LIBRARY-WIDE PRINT DEFECT FOUND BY DIAGNOSTIC — THE PRINT STANDARD
HAS NEVER TAKEN EFFECT IN ANY OF 71 FILES; CACHE v24 → v25; OI-58 OPENED &
CLOSED, OI-59, OI-60, OI-61–66 OPENED, OI-67 OPENED & CLOSED; next free OI-68).

THE OWNER CAUGHT A REAL GAP AND IT IS THE MOST IMPORTANT FINDING OF THE DAY.
The OI-59 reconciliation gate checked the working papers and the three audit
reports and pronounced the register substantially complete. IT NEVER OPENED R8.
R8 §2 carries a MONITOR block flagged by the workshop at the Apr 2026 service
(308,248 km) and a curated "Critical Outstanding" section — and NONE of it had
ever reached the Open Items Register. Two of those items are safety-relevant: a
rear hub seal weeping onto the shoes of the primary parking brake, and front
knuckle grease escaping from birfields that fail quickly once dry. Also
untracked: no thermostat on record in 316 entries, no water pump replacement on
record, a coolant flush ~86,006 km overdue, a wheel bearing repack ~63,684 km
overdue, and diff breather extensions nobody has looked for.
GENERAL LESSON: A COMPLETENESS GATE IS ONLY AS WIDE AS ITS POPULATION. The gate
was scoped to the instruments that LOOK like registers — spreadsheets and audit
reports — and missed a reference DOCUMENT that had quietly become one. R8 is a
dashboard; dashboards accumulate status. Any future reconciliation must sweep
for status-bearing content wherever it lives, not only where a register is
expected. This is the same failure shape as the philosophy-batch flag written
from recollection: the population was assumed rather than established.

WORKSHOP ADVISORIES NOW HAVE A HOME. They had none — the service ledger records
work PERFORMED, and its Notes column has never carried "the workshop said watch
X". A new ADVISORIES sheet is added to LC_Service_history_FINAL.xlsx: ID, date,
mileage, source, item, detail, severity, action, status, OI ref. Ten rows seeded
from R8, one already closed. THE ROWS WERE DELIBERATELY NOT ADDED TO THE
SERVICES SHEET — under the COST-0 convention a zero-cost row means "child line
item of the invoice above", so advisories there would corrupt the cost ledger
and both derived HTML ledgers.
STANDING CONVENTION ADOPTED: every service event asks what the workshop flagged,
and anything flagged is written to the Advisories sheet and, if it needs action,
carried as an OI. R8 remains the OWNING document for the detail; the register
holds a pointer.

AND THE SAME OWNER CATCH EXTENDED TO THE R8 SUPPLEMENT, WHICH IS WORSE. The
Service Checklist carries `var OUTSTANDING` — six items presented as live
workshop actions — HARDCODED WITH NO SOURCE FIELD, while the ALL_ITEMS array in
the SAME FILE carries src:'FSM'/'DOC'/'SH'/'BOTH' on every entry. The convention
existed and this array did not use it. Untraceable, therefore unmaintained,
therefore drifted: head studs still flagged for assessment after Udel ruled them
NOT REQUIRED in Jun 2026, and a boost gauge still flagged "verify fitted or
install" after the owner confirmed it fitted on 8 Jul 2026. This document has a
print-and-take-to-the-workshop function, so a stale CRITICAL flag spends money
re-doing settled work. OI-67.
THE THREE LEGITIMATE ENTRIES WERE TRACKED NOWHERE — rocker cover gasket, PCV
routing, Racor pre-filter. The first two are now OI-66, deliberately as ONE item
because the ORDER matters: verify the crankcase breather routing BEFORE
resealing the head. If the breather is routed post-intercooler, boost
pressurises the crankcase and forces oil past every top-end seal — reseal first
and the new gasket fails the same way.
GENERAL LESSON, PROMOTED TO A WORKING CONVENTION BELOW: A LIST WITHOUT
PROVENANCE CANNOT BE MAINTAINED. Every status-bearing array in this library
carries a source field or it becomes a liability with a print button.
ADVISORIES SHEET IS LIVE-ONLY (owner ruling, 29 Jul 2026): a closed advisory is
REMOVED, not retained — ADV-005 (fan belt quality) deleted on that basis, the
belts having been replaced since. IDs are not reused, so a gap is deliberate.
The durable lesson from a removed row lives in its owning document, not here.

WHY THIS SESSION. The owner opened it as a business question — how the library
might be monetised — and it turned into a usability finding, which is the more
valuable of the two. Working through what a productised version would contain,
he could not answer from his own library what the abbreviations meant, how to
use the Open Items Register, or whether the Controlled Duplication Register
existed. It does. It sits between Section E and the closed log. THE OWNER OF A
FOUR-YEAR SYSTEM COULD NOT NAVIGATE IT, AND THAT IS A DEFECT IN THE SYSTEM, NOT
IN HIM.

ROOT CAUSE, AND IT GENERALISES. The governance layer was built WRITE-OPTIMISED
FOR THE ASSISTANT, NOT READ-OPTIMISED FOR THE OWNER. Every instrument is a plain
.txt outside the PWA — unlinked from index.html, not precached, not searchable,
not printable. An assistant reads 108 KB instantly and joins three files in
working memory; the owner cannot, and should not have to. He has been the
proprietor of this system but never its operator. Confirmed against the Library
Register that no user guide existed: R30 answers "which document covers X",
index.html answers "I have a problem, where do I start", and nothing answered
"what is this machinery and how do I drive it".

A ONE-FACT-THREE-OWNERS VIOLATION, INSIDE THE LAYER THAT ENFORCES THE RULE. The
Readiness Actions live in three places: their descriptions in this file, their
status in the register, their tracking row in the working papers. Answering
"what is RA-5 and is it done?" requires two files and a mental join. Logged as
input to the dashboard work rather than fixed here.

THE PRINT FINDING IS THE LARGER ONE, AND IT CAME FROM A COMPLAINT ABOUT FONT
SIZE. Every font size in this library is declared in rem, which resolves against
the ROOT element. Every @media print block sets body. BODY IS NOT ROOT, so for
rem-sized text the print rule does nothing whatever, and never has. Depth-aware
diagnostic across all 71 HTML files at pinned HEAD: ZERO set the root size in
print; 1,547 rem declarations sit in base CSS. Printed pages have been coming
out at screen size since the print standard was written. Detail and remedy:
OI-60. GENERAL LESSON: A STANDARD THAT WAS NEVER MEASURED IS A HYPOTHESIS. DG
§16 has been cited as satisfied in session after session on the strength of the
rule being PRESENT in the file, which is not the same as it being IN EFFECT.

THE REMEDY IS A KNOB, NOT A TABLE OF SIZES. One root declaration inside @media
print, plus a shared block of rem heading overrides that COMPRESS the hierarchy
— on screen a heading earns its size, on paper it need only be distinguishable.
Because the overrides are rem rather than pt they do not fight the root, so a
single value rescales the whole document with relationships intact. Prototyped
on the guide: 11.6px → 8pt body, 13.0 → 9pt, 14.5 → 10pt, 17.4 → 12pt.

AND THE FIRST SIZE RULING WAS WRONG BECAUSE THE DIAGNOSIS WAS MIS-STATED. Claude
reported the body text as the cause of long printouts; the owner reasonably
ruled 12pt, printed it, and found it worse. Body prose was already printing at
11pt — the length was coming from 20.4pt section titles, a 33.6pt masthead and
1.65 line-height. THE MEASUREMENT WAS AVAILABLE BEFORE THE RULING WAS SOUGHT AND
WAS NOT TAKEN. Same class as the 29 Jul masthead-date error: an option offered is
already a claim, and an owner ruling on a defective option set produces a
defective ruling and costs a round trip. 8pt adopted on re-ruling.

SECTIONS 03, 06 AND 08 OF THE GUIDE ARE WRITTEN FOR MARY-ANN. This is an RA-8
input, not a courtesy. Solo capability against a sixty-document library with five
register categories and twelve abbreviation families requires an entry point
assuming no prior knowledge; the library had been written throughout on the
assumption that its reader was the owner or the assistant.

TWO OWNER GATES RULED. Classification: infrastructure class, no R-number,
masthead-EXEMPT under DG S10 — but carrying a Last-updated line by DELIBERATE
DIVERGENCE, because unlike the Design Guidelines this document goes stale
whenever the library's shape changes. Recorded here and in the Library Register
so a later session does not "correct" it. Placement: first card in index.html §2
Find Anything, ahead of R30 — crisis-first is a stress-state principle and a
manual does not outrank Emergency.

FOUR PROBE ERRORS, ALL CAUGHT. The instructive one searched for this file's
PHRASING of the duplication register rather than the register's actual table
FORMAT, returned zero hits, and nearly reported that the register did not exist.
Also: a "max-width:1" guard firing on the correct "max-width:100%"; a status
extractor reading four entries as blank because they use varied vocabulary
("DORMANT by design", "SUBSTANTIALLY CLOSED") rather than a keyword; and an
over-broad print classifier whose "OK" category dissolved on spot-checking
against computed sizes. SUSPECT THE PROBE FIRST, four for four.

THE FLAG WAS WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, AND MEASUREMENT SETTLED IT. It named

mis-stated a population in both directions at once.

ROOT CAUSE: AN UNFINISHED MIGRATION, NOT A DEFECT. S10's Migration status
block groups "philosophy + itinerary" as ONE family batch. The itinerary half

a state the standard explicitly anticipates and tolerates — which reframes the

SCOPE WAS THREE SLOTS. Beyond the absent date, all four carried a non-standard
eyebrow and had substituted editorial prose for the invariant identity line.
A date-only pass would have shipped two live divergences under a conformance
claim.

A RULING WAS REVERSED ON EVIDENCE, AND THE ERROR WAS MINE. The owner first
ruled that each file carry its true last-CONTENT-edit date rather than the
session date — on my proposal. Reading S10 at HEAD then showed the standard
ALREADY prescribes the session date for migrated files: I had offered an open
sub-gate on a question the standard had settled, having proposed before
verifying, which inverts working convention (c). Independently the ruling was
unexecutable: commit messages in this repo are bare ("commit", "comit") and
the most recent commit on every one of these files is the 29 Jul CSS-only TOC
pass, so a "true content date" could only have come from diff-adjudication
across five-plus revisions — a number asserted rather than read, F65 class.
GENERAL LESSON: AN OPTION OFFERED IS ALREADY A CLAIM. Convention (c) says
verify a finding before writing it down; it applies with equal force to the
OPTIONS in a proposal, because an owner ruling on a defective option set
produces a defective ruling and costs a round trip to unwind.

THE EDITORIAL LINE WAS KEPT, AND THE PLACEMENT DEVIATES FROM THE PRECEDENT
IT CITES. The four glosses ("written to be read at the fire") are good
writing and S10's own rationale — the masthead identifies the DOCUMENT, it
does not re-specify the vehicle — cuts against stamping a turbo spec onto a
manifesto. They are retained as a Philosophy family extra element on the
profile native-name precedent. But profiles place that element directly under
the TITLE, and porting that here was measured and rejected: the profile
.std-sub is a 0.92rem sans italic while the philosophy .std-sub is a 0.54rem
mono amber line — typographically the SAME instrument as the profile's
.std-local — so the profile placement stacks two near-identical lines. The
gloss therefore stays below the subtitle, and S10 records the reason so the
divergence is not later "corrected" into the defect.

PRIOR (29 July 2026, twenty-second session) (LIBRARY-WIDE TOC WRAP
DEFECT CLOSED — THE SINGLE-ROW SCROLLER IS NOW A BASE RULE IN 33 FILES AND
MANDATORY BY DESIGN GUIDELINES v1.8; 39 FILES EDITED ACROSS FOUR PASSES;
A DEPTH-AWARE RE-CLASSIFICATION DISSOLVED AN ENTIRE CATEGORY AND PREVENTED
13 UNNECESSARY EDITS; OI-54 OPENED & CLOSED; next free OI-55).

WHY THIS SESSION. The owner opened it with a UI complaint, not a governance
task: the Karoo itinerary's TOC wrapped to three rows on a laptop while
behaving correctly on mobile. Chasing it produced a LIBRARY-WIDE defect and a
Design Guidelines change. The first diagnosis — "my regression, I added two
buttons" — was WRONG and was corrected: the two extra buttons pushed one file
over a threshold, but the wrap was structural and pre-existing in 29 files.

ROOT CAUSE, ONE LINE. The single-row scroller was scoped to a max-width media
query, so desktop fell through to a base rule declaring flex-wrap:wrap. The
TOC sits inside the mandatory sticky unit, so every wrapped row permanently
consumed viewport on every screen of the document.

THE FIX WAS ALREADY IN THE LIBRARY. Four files (R4, R8, R19, Seal Gasket
Atlas) carried the scroller in the BASE rule. R4, with 75 TOC buttons and a
~15,400px row, works precisely because of it. The remedy was therefore
ADOPTION of a proven in-house pattern, and the one thing Claude invented — a
slim @media (pointer:fine) scrollbar for mouse users — was offered, REJECTED
by the owner in favour of consistency, and reverted out of the Karoo file
before the programme started. Do not reintroduce it.

FOUR PASSES, 39 FILES. S1 Profile TEMPLATE + Design Guidelines — template
FIRST, so no future profile inherits the defect (the OI-36 lesson), and the
standard written into DG S6 with the document bumped v1.7 → v1.8. S2 ten
country/terrain profiles, with changelog entries in BOTH formats per the
OI-39 divergence. S3 six remaining overflowing files. S4 ten latent ones.
Closing corpus assert passes on all 61 files: none wraps, none lacks a scroll
mechanism. 33 carry the standard explicitly; 28 are correct by CSS default
and were LEFT UNTOUCHED — editing working files for cosmetic uniformity is
churn, and every edit is a chance to break something.

FOUR PROBE ERRORS, ALL CAUGHT, AND THE FOURTH IS THE INSTRUCTIVE ONE. (1) The
first survey matched only <nav class="toc"> and reported ONE affected file;
most of the library uses .toc-bar. (2) The idempotence guard fired on Angola
for overscroll-behavior-x:contain — which also sits on .table-scroll under
the standing inner-scroller pattern. (3) The changelog anchor assumed a
wrapper style that varies between profiles. (4) THE CATEGORY CLASSIFIER TOOK
THE FIRST .toc-bar MATCH IN THE FLATTENED CSS, but in many files that match
is inside a media query — Brake System Guide's first match is a mobile
padding rule, Vehicle Recovery's is the print rule. Rebuilt with DEPTH-AWARE
parsing (a rule is BASE only when braces balance to zero before it), which
DISSOLVED an entire category: the 13 files classed "no scroll mechanism" were
already correct, and S4 fell from 23 files to 10. Thirteen healthy files
would have been edited for nothing. GENERAL RULE: a CSS probe must respect
NESTING, not text order — flattening the stylesheet destroys the very scoping
that decides which rule applies.

THE ANCHOR THAT WOULD HAVE DONE REAL DAMAGE. flex-wrap:wrap appears 2–8 times
per file, almost always on OTHER components — .xref-bar, .comp-header,
.ohm-tri, .cover-specs, .footer, .sec-header. Spares & Tools alone has eight.
A replace on that string would have silently unwrapped half a dozen unrelated
layouts per file. The safe anchor was ';flex-wrap:wrap;border-bottom'
(count==1 everywhere), and validation asserts the OTHER wrap rules survive.

FLAGGED, NOT FIXED. Five files carry no masthead "Last updated" line — the
Manifesto, the Commentary, History of Overlanding and Expedition as a System
(all Philosophy, which IS in the DG S10 closed vocabulary) plus the Content
Audit (governance, legitimately exempt). Same class as the Karoo masthead
ruled on 28 Jul; not fixed silently. Separately, non-profile R-docs have no
changelog section, so the "every masthead bump carries a changelog entry"
rule cannot be honoured for them — it needs an exemption or changelogs.

nav, table, tbody, tr, dl, pre), ids unique, node --check clean, TOC index

THE SHARPEST LEAK WAS NOT THE ITINERARY TABLE. It was DARK SKIES, which mapped

is bright in the middle. GENERAL LESSON: a date-suppression sweep must look for
the ANCHORING, not the date format — anything that lets a reader pin Day N to a
calendar day is in scope, including public astronomical facts used as a key.

RA-1 DE-DATING WAS NEVER ACTUALLY COMPLETED — the session's most useful finding,
and a repeat of a lesson already in this file. The 22 Jul undating ruling was
remediated on 24 Jul against a NAMED LIST (R29 §2, R11, Facts §2). A corpus
sweep this session found the departure-dated deadline STILL LIVE against RA-1 in
THREE files: R11 (a SECOND, differently-worded instance the 24 Jul fix did not
match), R23 and R38 (never in scope at all). The 24 Jul fix swept its change
spec, not the corpus, and matched one literal string form rather than the fact.
Same class as the eighteenth session's "77" and the RC1 pass's abbreviated-name
form — three sessions, three recurrences, which is why the rule is stated in the
imperative: THE INSTANCES YOU LIST ARE NOT THE INSTANCES THAT EXIST. Facts was
correct throughout and needed only coarsening.

RESEARCH THAT COULD NOT BE INVENTED. The owner asked for nearest Toyota dealers
as breakdown cover. Six were verified with addresses and numbers — and the

dealer in any direction. §17 therefore names a Calvinia tyre-and-repair shop as
the realistic first call for that leg. A Victoria West listing was EXCLUDED
because it reads as a branded filling station rather than a service dealer, and
the Gannabos coordinate is published as "confirm on booking" because the source
data is internally inconsistent by ~20 km. Pass specifications (Ouberg's
gradients, Gannaga's bend count, the winter-snow warning) are cited from
mountain-pass references, not recalled — F65 discipline in a document that now
carries driving-hazard advice.

PRIOR (28 July 2026, twentieth session) — WHY THAT SESSION. A public-exposure review of the repo, opened by the owner.
The finding was not any single fact but an AGGREGATION: the register number,
the previous plate, the first-licensed and certificate-issue dates, the series
name as registered, the vehicle description, the registered owner's full legal
name, the region, the insurer, both medical schemes and a dated two-year
absence window all sit in one public repository. Individually each is minor.
Assembled against a distinctive vehicle they form a usable package for
registration fraud and for knowing precisely when a South African house stands
empty and for how long. The existing personal-data rule kept ID numbers out of
committed files; the bar for a public repo is higher than that.

MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED. The item list was swept at pinned HEAD before any spec
was written (118 files, full-corpus tarball) — working convention (c), verify
before you write it down. The sweep CHANGED the job in both directions. It
found LESS than feared: no ID numbers anywhere, every membership and policy
field already a blank fill-in, and the commit author name a generic org handle
with no email exposed. It also found MORE: Facts §1 carried two fields nobody
had named — the PREVIOUS licence number and the certificate issue date and
office, the latter being the only place the library stated the home city as an
administrative fact tied to the owner's name.

THE RULING THAT SHAPED THE EDIT. Full legal names go offline; FIRST NAMES STAY.
Evidence decided it: every full-name instance in a library HTML file was the
same sentence — "the vehicle is registered to <full name>" — where the surname
does no work, while first names run to ~280 instances across 40+ files and
carry real readability. A first name is not a join key. The full legal name,
paired with the vehicle and the region, is.

GATES (owner-ruled 28 Jul): history NOT rewritten — a filter-repo pass would
rewrite every SHA and break the SHA-pinning convention the entire working
method rests on, for material that is either self-expiring (dates) or visible
on the vehicle (plate); redaction is forward-looking only and the residual is
accepted knowingly. RC1 doctrine SPLIT rather than kept or wholly retired.
Tare RETAINED as a technical weight figure with live RA-1 value — and because
it sat INSIDE the certificate block, it was promoted out first, so §1 would not
read as a pointer followed by one orphaned field inviting restoration. Karoo
dates and the personal mary-ann-70/ page DEFERRED by owner decision, recorded
in the annex as decisions rather than omissions.

ARCHIVES: REDACTION IS NOT RECONSTRUCTION. The Open Items Register and Session
History are archives, and the standing doctrine is that history is not
reconstructed. Withdrawing a VALUE is a different act from altering a RECORD —
but a reader who finds a pointer where a number used to be must be told why.
Both files therefore carry a DATED REDACTION NOTICE stating what class of data
was withdrawn, that no narrative was altered, removed or reconstructed, and
that prior commits still hold the originals. An archive that has been redacted
says so; otherwise the redaction itself becomes the invisible seam.

THE SWEEP EARNED ITS PLACE AGAIN — the same lesson as the eighteenth session's
"77". The change spec named an abbreviated initial-plus-surname form of the owner name in ONE file. The
post-edit corpus sweep found FIVE instances across FOUR files, including
Zimbabwe, which was not in the spec at all and would have shipped carrying the
owner reference under a pass that claimed to have removed it. Zimbabwe took a
masthead bump and a revision row of its own. TWO COUNT GUARDS FIRED AND BOTH
WERE CORRECT: one file was asserted at 2 instances and held 3 (grep LINE counts
had been used as OCCURRENCE counts — wrong wherever one line carries two hits),
and the re-run guard refused to double-apply. Neither became a no-op dressed as
success.

ONE DEVIATION FROM THE APPROVED SPEC, DISCLOSED. The spec said full name →
"the registered owner". In place most instances read "registered to <full
name>", which would have produced "registered to the registered owner" —
circular. The surname was dropped instead: identical security outcome, since
the full legal name is the join key, without rewriting sentences.

A PROBE ERROR, DISCLOSED, SAME CLASS AS THE id="changelog" CASE. The cross-file
next-free check first reported max OI-47 and sixteen sequence gaps. That was the
probe, not the file: it matched only line-start "OI-nn Title" definitions, which
by design sees OPEN items only, while closed items live in the closed log under
a "date OI-nn · TITLE" form. The "gaps" were closed items. OI-51 was confirmed
genuinely unassigned on the corrected run — an EARMARK IS NOT AN ASSIGNMENT, and
reserving a live number against a contingent finding is what produced the OI-46
collision. When a probe disagrees with the file, suspect the probe.

SECOND TRANCHE, SAME SESSION — LICENCE AND DISC DATES. The exact card and
disc expiry dates were withdrawn to PRIVATE ANNEX §3 and COARSENED to
month/year in place (32 instances, 6 files). Coarsened rather than pointed,
because R29, R38 and OI-24 reason about RELATIVE timing — which card expires
inside the expedition window and which after the return — and a bare pointer
would have broken that reasoning in a life-safety and insurance-validity
context. The exact DAY is what lets a caller sound credible; the MONTH is all
the planning needs. Facts §11 keeps the coarse months and points at the annex
for the day. CLASSIFICATION CORRECTED: the cover / medical-scheme cluster was
provisionally graded Class R in the opening review and is REGRADED CLASS P —
RETAIN. ~230 instances sit in the R29/R10/R1/R16 emergency stack, where a
paramedic or a bystander holding the laminated R1 card needs to know which
scheme to claim against. Redacting it would trade a nuisance call against a
delayed evacuation. The residual is controlled BEHAVIOURALLY, in R35's
standing habits, not by redaction. A classification made before measurement
is a hypothesis; this one was corrected by measuring what the strings do.

PRIOR (26 July 2026, eighteenth session) (LiFePO4 CALCULATOR GAINS
THE MULTI-DAY STAY BLOCK; DAILY-LOAD FIGURE SUPERSEDED 77 → 65.3 Ah/day
ACROSS R5/R7/CALCULATOR; CALCULATOR DC-DC SPEC CORRECTED 30A → 40A;
OI-48 AND OI-49 OPENED AND CLOSED SAME DAY; next free OI-50).

SESSION ORIGIN — AN EXTERNAL VIDEO USED AS AN AUDIT INSTRUMENT. The owner
brought an Overland Workshop (Aspyr) video on electrical-system design and
asked how it sat against the library. Claude cannot watch video; the owner
supplied the transcript, which is the correct division of labour. The
video's central claim — that a lithium bank connected to an alternator
WITHOUT a DC-DC charger can draw enough current to burn the alternator out
— independently corroborates the rationale for the Orion ~40 A cap
(R7/R26). Graded V. On the 1HZ, whose alternator is smaller than the
120–180 A European units the presenter cites, the cap matters MORE, not
less. His alternator-upgrade suggestion is a path R31 already rejected and
RA-4 ruled on (24 Jul, no spare carried). His solar pessimism is NOT
imported: it comes from a 400 Ah mission with Starlink and an electric
oven, which is not ours.

WHAT THE VIDEO EXPOSED was not a doctrine error but a GAP: the library
modelled a single day well and a multi-day STAY not at all. R7's "~2.2 days
wild-camp autonomy" is a ZERO-INPUT floor — it ignores solar entirely — so
it badly understates a real stationary stay with the blanket out, and it
was the only multi-day number in the library. The owner's question ("if we
stay 3 days, or 5, what do we need to do?") had no tool that answered it.

MULTI-DAY STAY BLOCK BUILT (LiFePO4 Calculator). Four inputs — days at stop
1–14 · arrival SOC · sun scenario (good 5.5 h / average 4.0 h / poor 2.5 h
peak-sun-hours) · recharge-drive length (default 3 h). Outputs — Energy In
/ Out / Net triptych, a day-by-day SOC projection table with green/amber/
red banding, and a verdict naming how many recharge drives a stay needs and
on which days. OWNER-RATIFIED MODEL: stationary days are solar-only (engine
off, so no alternator term); a recharge drive fires ONLY on a day the bank
would otherwise breach the 20% floor — the owner's "drive only when needed"
doctrine, NOT a fixed every-N-days cadence. The SOC trajectory carries no
loss factor, matching the existing single-day maths so the two cannot
disagree.

HYBRID CHOSEN OVER A STANDALONE REPORT, AND THE REASON IS THE SEAM RULE.
The owner supplied the RETIRED R6 Battery Longevity Report as the visual
reference and asked for "something like this". R6 was a standalone static
report; rebuilding that shape would have added a file (CACHE bump, index,
register, R30, a new R-number) AND created a SECOND OWNER of a calculation
the LiFePO4 Calculator already owns — the exact structural weakness the
content-audit programme spent five sessions closing. The block therefore
EXTENDS the calculator, borrowing R6's In/Out/Net card presentation and
NONE of its figures (R6 is 200/300 Ah bank, 80/110 A alternator, 30 A Orion
era — every number in it is superseded).

DAILY-LOAD FIGURE SUPERSEDED — 77 → 65.3 Ah/day (OI-48, opened and closed
same day). Found by SIMULATING the new block against the calculator's own
lc76 load preset, which summed to 65.3 while R7's headline said 77 and R7's
own autonomy figure implied ~69. Three numbers for one fact. THE DIAGNOSIS
IS THE INTERESTING PART: the breakdowns were right and the headline was
wrong. R5's itemised table AND the calculator's reference-load callout both
already summed to 65.3 — the "77" total sat on top of correct component
lists in two separate files and had never been reconciled to them. OWNER
RULING: 65.3 authoritative, no Starlink carried. All corrections move in
the FAVOURABLE direction — longer autonomy, lower DoD, break-even solar in
full sun, longer battery life.

TWO MODEL ARTIFACTS DELIBERATELY NOT RE-DERIVED (owner-agreed), because
recomputing them would have meant inventing numbers: (a) R7's capacity-fade
curve was modelled at the higher DoD, so at 31% it is now CONSERVATIVE —
real retention should meet or exceed it. A basis note saying exactly that
was added to R7; the curve was NOT recomputed, because a replacement needs
Enertec DoD-vs-cycle-life data and an invented curve reads as authority it
has not earned (F65 class). (b) R5's day-by-day autonomy table runs on a
deliberately conservative ~41 Ah/day net (shade, dust, winter sun) DECOUPLED
from the headline load, so it stays valid; a one-line pointer to the
Multi-day Stay block was added so a reader does not mistake the two
readings for a contradiction.

THE SWEEP EARNED ITS PLACE — STANDING LESSON. The planned anchor list
covered 18 instances of "77"; a post-edit corpus sweep found FIVE MORE,
including R5's load-breakdown TABLE TOTAL. Had the session stopped at the
planned edits, R5 would have carried a 65.3 headline above a table still
totalling 77. The instances you LIST are not the instances that EXIST; the
sweep is not optional and must run wider than the change spec. TWO GUARDS
FIRED AND BOTH WERE CORRECT: "~2.2 days" appeared TWICE in R7 (§1 spec
table and §4 summary) and the count==1 assert refused a blind replace —
disambiguated by row context, both changed deliberately; and the calculator
spec-strip assert refused because the target string is split across <span>
tags, i.e. the flattened-text search had misread the markup. Neither
failure became a no-op dressed as success.

SEPARATE PRE-EXISTING DEFECT FIXED IN THE SAME PASS: the calculator masthead
spec strip read "DC–DC: 30A Orion" while the tool's own slider defaults to
40 A and Facts specifies the Orion Smart 12/12-50A capped ~40 A. Corrected
to 40 A. Unrelated to the load question — found only because the stale
sweep ran wide. No OI consumed: a spec contradiction against Facts is a
defect, not a decision.

GOVERNANCE-DELIVERABLE DEFECT, DISCLOSED — AND THE RULE IT RESTORES. This
session first delivered the PI and OIR updates as PATCH DOCUMENTS
(PI_UPDATE / OIR_UPDATE .txt) describing edits for the owner to apply by
hand, on the reasoning that regenerating a large governance file risks
silent drift. The owner correctly challenged it: a patch file committed to
the repo is an ORPHAN of the OI-32/OI-42 class, it updates neither the
GitHub file nor the project-instructions field, and it hands the owner an
error-prone manual step. THE DRIFT CONCERN WAS REAL BUT THE REMEDY WAS
WRONG. The correct method is the one already used for every HTML file:
apply anchored str.replace edits PROGRAMMATICALLY to the live file and
deliver the actual updated .txt. STANDING RULE RESTORED AND SHARPENED:
governance files are delivered as COMPLETE FILES, never as patch
instructions; where the file is large, the protection against drift is
anchored programmatic editing with count==1 asserts and a before/after
structural reconciliation — not hand-application by the owner. The two
patch files must be deleted and never committed.

SAME-SESSION ADDENDUM (27 Jul 2026) — PROJECT-KNOWLEDGE FOSSILS REMOVED
(OI-49, opened and closed same day). Verifying that the new Project
Instructions had reached the instructions FIELD turned up three stale
governance copies sitting in project KNOWLEDGE: Facts 20.6 KB dated
18 Jun (live 28.4 KB, 24 Jul), Library Register 18.6 KB dated 14 Jun
(live 38.8 KB, 10 Jul), and Project Instructions 14.3 KB dated 23 Jun
against a 200.8 KB live file — the fossil was SEVEN PER CENT of the real

not yet caused an error only because the fetch-live-SHA-pinned
convention was suppressing it, which is a discipline holding back a
hazard rather than the absence of one — and the routing rule sending
"Facts.txt queries" to the fastest tier is exactly the path on which a
six-week-old Facts returns a confident wrong answer with no fetch. All
three REMOVED by the owner the same day. PREFER ABSENCE TO STALENESS: a
copy that does not exist forces a fetch; a copy that exists and is wrong
invites a stale read and reports no error.

REPO ACCESS INTERRUPTION, RECORDED BECAUSE IT AFFECTS THE WORKING METHOD:

unauthenticated-API 403. The session STOPPED rather than working from
project-knowledge or memory copies, which is the correct behaviour and is
why R7 and the calculator could not simply be "recalled". Access returned

PRIOR (24 July 2026, sixteenth session) (RA-4 RULED — NO SPARE
ALTERNATOR CARRIED; PROPAGATED THROUGH FOUR OWNING DOCUMENTS).
OWNER RULING: a complete spare alternator will NOT be carried. Recorded
rationale — the 70-Series alternator is a commonly available part along
the route, the unit is heavy and expensive, and payload stays PROVISIONAL
until RA-1. Carried redundancy is the brush/regulator kit, the starter
contact/solenoid kit and a spare V-belt set, with a replacement sourced
en route. OI-45 CLOSED; OI-46 opened; next free is OI-47.

WHAT THE RULING ACTUALLY CHANGES, written down because it is not
obvious from the ruling itself: the alternator is the crank battery's
ONLY charge source. Solar charges the house bank. Shore power through the
MultiPlus charges the house bank. The Orion is one-way, crank to house.
Starting off the LiFePO4 is prohibited — no paralleling path, house
wiring not rated for cranking current, BMS will not tolerate the draw. So
when the alternator fails, every charging path into the Willard fails
with it, and the ARB jump pack stops being a convenience and becomes the
only thing between the crew and an immobile vehicle. That is now doctrine
in R32, and it is why OI-46 asks a cheap question with a large
consequence: can the jump pack itself be recharged in the field off the
house bank? If yes, the survival chain is solar → house → pack → crank
and it must be written down. If no, the ruling leaves a hole.

R41 C1 REWRITTEN AND A CONTRADICTION REMOVED. The cascade's chain-breaker
moved from "swap the unit" to "detect early, limp somewhere recoverable,
source en route", which promotes the R32 §8 checks — especially AC ripple
for a failing diode pack — from routine to load-bearing, and records that
the kit does NOT fix a diode pack. Separately, C1 had carried "Emergency
bridge: the house bank can jump the crank side in extremis", which
DIRECTLY CONTRADICTED R32's explicit prohibition. Two documents
disagreeing on a life-of-the-vehicle procedure. Removed; R41 points at
R32 as owner.

R31 CORRECTED — THE LIBRARY HAD BEEN ASSERTING A SPARE THAT DOES NOT
EXIST. Its alternator row stated "the refurbished stock unit also serves
as the field spare". That was a leftover from the REJECTED high-output
alternator case: declining the upgrade means there was never a displaced
unit. The claim sat in one document and was propagated nowhere — not to
R3, which is where a carried spare belongs, not to Facts, not to R32.
Exactly the marooned-fact pattern the content audit named. R3 gained item
4.7 Alternator Brush & Regulator Kit; belts (1.3) and the starter
solenoid (4.6) were already carried.

PROCESS DEFECT DISCLOSED. An edit script re-run after two partial anchor
failures applied the R32 insertion TWICE. The assert count==1 guard could
not catch it because the anchor survived inside its own replacement, so
the guard passed on every run. Detected by a post-edit count, R32
restored from the pinned SHA and re-applied once. STANDING LESSON:
assert count==1 protects the ANCHOR, not IDEMPOTENCE. Any script that
might be re-run must also assert the NEW content is ABSENT before
writing. This is the same lesson as encode-before-truncate — the failure
was in the edit pattern, not in the validation that follows it.

CLEAN END TO END: R29 §6 owns the master set, R10 §9 is aligned, R1
carries the printed card, R16 is corrected, and R38 was swept and

advance by the Controlled Duplication Register; five accounted for.

SAME-SESSION ADDENDUM — OPEN ITEMS REGISTER RE-FILED. Housekeeping on

SECOND SELF-AUDIT CATCH IN THE SAME PASS, AND THE MORE IMPORTANT ONE:
the register had grown TWO "CLOSED LOG" headers. The Session 2 edit
spliced in a block that already carried the header and then re-appended
the original, orphaning every entry from 22 Jul and earlier under a
second heading — committed and pushed twice before it was seen. Merged
into one log, 41 entries, newest-first, none lost. It was caught only
because an integrity probe written for the RE-FILE asserted that header
appeared exactly once. THE LESSON IS ABOUT THE PIPELINE, NOT THE DEFECT:
this edit passed every gate the validation pipeline runs, because depth
walks and node --check are HTML instruments and say nothing about
whether a plain-text governance file has sprouted a duplicate heading.
Governance .txt files are edited by splice as often as the HTML is, and
they currently have no structural gate at all. Worth adding one: assert
each section header appears exactly once and that item IDs are unique,
whenever the OIR or Facts is spliced. NOTED FOR THE
OWNER, AND CORRECTED: the first version of this note said RA-4 was "the
one" readiness action with no open item of its own. WRONG — RA-8 was in
the same position. The error came from checking item TITLES rather than
item bodies, the same shortcut that produced the character-count error
recorded above, and it was caught the same way, by checking. BOTH ARE NOW
RESOLVED, and the difference between them decided the treatment. RA-8 is
welded to the Karoo post-trip review, a dated event weeks away, so it
surfaces on its own — it stays inside OI-13, which was retitled "R23
post-trip review + RA-8" so that a scan finds it. RA-4 rode inside EX-8,
which carries NO trigger date, and an action parked inside an undated
workstream is invisible until that workstream runs: if EX-8 slips, RA-4
slips silently with it. It was given OI-45 and filed under DECISIONS, not
actions, because the first question is a genuine owner ruling — complete
spare alternator versus brush/regulator plus starter kit, a trade-off in
weight, cost and field skill, and payload-coupled to RA-1. It is also the
designated chain-breaker for R41 cascade C1, which is why it earns
visibility disproportionate to its cost. On ruling it moves to section A
and its parts lines land in the R3 ledger via EX-8.

SELF-AUDIT CATCH, DISCLOSED. The first draft of the OIR closing entry
quoted R1 and R16 end-of-edit character counts of 33,392 and 54,930.
Both were wrong — estimated, not measured — and were caught by checking
them against the files before the entry stood. Corrected to 32,864 and
54,157. The starting figures were right, which is exactly what makes
this dangerous: a paragraph of accurate numbers is where an invented one
hides. Same class as F65, and working convention (d) is what caught it.

PRIOR (24 July 2026, fourteenth session — SESSIONS 2 AND 3 EXECUTED). The 23 Jul handover pack is fully discharged. Session 2:
R29 gained the emergency-number correction (M1a + M1b), §13 Vehicle
Asset Cover (M2) and the medical-layer decoupling doctrine (M3).
Session 3: R10 gained the corrected numbers and the drill content (M4)

call-first-argue-cover-later doctrine. Run in the correct order,
owner first: R29 owns the stack, R10 delegates to it.

R10 §9 NOW CARRIES THE CALL SCRIPT, which is the single highest-value
thing added in either session. Five items, invariant order: who is
calling plus the callback number · what has happened in ONE sentence ·
exact position read aloud off the InReach and confirmed back · casualty
count and condition · what is being requested, said explicitly. It
exists because under stress people narrate instead of reporting and
forget the coordinates. Alongside it, CALL FIRST, ARGUE COVER LATER,
with the reasoning written down rather than asserted: the failure mode
is a careful person behaving carefully — a prudent operator wants to
confirm entitlement before committing to a cost, and every minute spent
confirming it is a minute the casualty does not have. Entitlement is
recoverable after the fact; physiology is not. R10 points at the R29 §6
four-layer model rather than restating it — one-owner rule upheld.

THE CONTROLLED DUPLICATION REGISTER HAD ITS FIRST LIVE USE AND WORKED.
Its emergency-numbers row names four delegating documents; all four were
visited or explicitly deferred, none left to memory. R10 aligned this
session; R1 deferred to OI-41 (it carries no numbers at all); R16
deferred to OI-44 (it carries a conflicting, unprovenanced one); R38
SWEPT AND VERIFIED to carry zero emergency numbers and zero provider
names, so it correctly needs no edit. That last one matters as method:
the register named R38, the sweep checked it, and "no change needed" is
a finding that was proved rather than assumed.

PROCESS DEFECT DISCLOSED — AND THE PATTERN CHANGED BECAUSE OF IT. An
emoji written as a lone surrogate pair threw on encode AFTER
open(...,'w') had already truncated the target file to ZERO BYTES. No
data was lost: R10 was restored from the pinned SHA in a single fetch,
which is precisely what the SHA-pinning discipline exists for. The edit
pattern is now hardened and this is a STANDING CHANGE — encode the
string FIRST, then write binary, so a bad encode can never again destroy
the target before it fails. A validation gate that runs after the write
cannot protect a file the write itself has emptied.

COMMIT-STATE VERIFICATION IS NOW PART OF RE-PINNING. Between the two
sessions the owner reported the Session 2 files as committed; a
cache-busted check of the raw main-branch copy showed they were NOT yet
landed — same byte count as the original, none of the four expected
markers present. The session waited rather than editing against a stale
HEAD, and on the second report the landing was verified file by file
before any edit. Recorded because "committed" is a claim like any other
and the cost of believing it wrongly is an edit built on the wrong
base.

M1b WAS EXECUTED, NOT DEFERRED. The pack blocked M1b on OI-40 because
inventing an international equivalent for a 6-digit SA short code is a
fabricated number in a life-safety document. The owner supplied the real
ones mid-session, read from the providers' own websites: Netcare 911
Emergency Operations Centre +27 11 301 0000 and +27 10 209 8911 (24/7,
an operator available after hours on either line); ER24 international
dispatch +27 10 205 3000 and Aeromedical / Flight Desk +27 10 205 3588,
both reaching the 24/7 ER24 Contact Centre. OI-40 opened and closed the
same day. GRADE RECORDED HONESTLY AND IT MATTERS: these are
PROVIDER-PUBLISHED, NOT VOICE-TESTED. A number that has never been
dialled on the equipment that will dial it is an assumption, not a
control, so a test-dial from the IsatPhone is added to the RA-10 regime
(OI-3), and until that is signed off AMREF remains the only medevac
number with a CONFIRMED satellite route. R29 §6 says exactly this on the
page rather than presenting all four numbers as equally proven.

APPEND-NOT-RENUMBER, DECIDED ON EVIDENCE. §13 Vehicle Asset Cover was
appended rather than inserted after §2 where it thematically belongs.
The premise was measured, not assumed: a corpus sweep found R29's
section numbers ARE externally cited — §7 ×11, §11 ×11, §2 ×3, §8 ×3,
§9 ×1, across R10, R11, R16, R38, R41, Facts and this file — so
renumbering would have broken every one. This is the exact MIRROR IMAGE
of the OI-36 profile decision, where the same sweep returned zero
references and renumbering was therefore right. THE RULE IS NOT "ALWAYS
APPEND" OR "ALWAYS RENUMBER" — IT IS "MEASURE FIRST, THEN CHOOSE". Both
outcomes are now on the record, two days apart, from the same test.

DISCLOSED EDIT BEYOND THE PACK'S SCOPE — AND THE REASON IT MATTERS. The

OI-1 and NEVER PROPAGATED INTO THE LIBRARY. Two days later R29 §2, R11
and Facts §2 all still instructed the reader to weigh the vehicle

with an explicit never-re-attach-a-date instruction. Corrected in all

the PROVISIONAL clause on kerb and payload. R37's own dated row was checked
and correctly LEFT ALONE — that is RA-7, the recovery practice day, a
different action whose date stands. Recorded prominently because it is
precisely the structural pattern the content-audit programme named: a
decision minuted in governance that never reaches the documents a reader
actually opens. The Project Instructions being right is not the same as
the library being right.

OI-37 ALSO CLOSED THE SAME DAY — the freshness-marker rollout was carried
through the whole family, not just the five new §06 sections. Every
section carrying prices, fees or security posture now opens with a dated
"Data verified" marker on the R35 §8 V/M/S convention. THE SECTION SET IS
FIXED AND UNIFORM: Entry & Visas · Border Crossings · Vehicle Documents ·

holding no perishable data. The set was chosen by judgement: a wrong
number in these sections costs money at a border, a park gate or a pump.
THE TEMPLATE CARRIES THE MARKER SET TOO, with a [DATE] placeholder, so a
new profile inherits it — the §06 retrofit existed only because the

marked sections instead of an open-ended instruction to refresh the
profiles. Next free open item OI-40.

OI-38 CLOSURE WAS WRONG AND HAS BEEN CORRECTED — READ THIS BEFORE
TRUSTING THE PARAGRAPH THAT FOLLOWS IT. The TOC contract was taken to be
"sectionIds mirrors the TOC buttons" and applied family-wide on that
basis. That is only half the contract; the other half is the TOGGLE
EXPRESSION consuming the pairing, and it was NOT uniform. 001–006 and the
template use i===idx; 007–010 used (i-1)===idx, under which an array that
deliberately OMITS the leading Alerts button is CORRECT. So 007, 008 and
009 were right before the "fix" and were BROKEN BY IT, and shipped to the
repo in that state. 005 was genuinely broken and correctly fixed; 010 was
genuinely broken and untouched. ROOT CAUSE: a family-wide rule asserted
from a sample without reading the consuming code per file — the F65 error
class exactly. REPAIRED the same day by NORMALISING rather than reverting:
the toggle in 007–010 changed to i===idx, which makes the already-altered
arrays correct and fixes 010 as well. All eleven files verified together on
one rule. The paragraph below is the original, over-confident account and
is retained for the record.

OI-38 OPENED AND CLOSED THE SAME DAY. The TOC/IntersectionObserver index
contract was swept across all ten profiles plus the template. FOUR files
were genuinely broken, all on the same 'alerts' mismatch — 007 Mozambique,
008 Malawi and 009 Zimbabwe each carried an Alerts button absent from the
array; 005 Kalahari had the inverse. All four repaired and mastheads
bumped; 010 Kenya was already correct and was NOT touched, so the defect
was not universal — the prediction logged when OI-38 was opened (that all
four July profiles would carry it) was WRONG, and is recorded as wrong.
Six files had been repaired earlier the same day during the OI-36 build,
so all eleven now hold the contract. Judgement call disclosed: mastheads
bumped but NO per-profile changelog entries added, on the grounds that a
silent-bug fix gives a reader nothing to notice and 007/008/009 do not
carry the changelog anchor format used elsewhere — owner to confirm
whether every touched file should carry an entry regardless.

R3 REVERSED — APPEND BECAME INSERT, ON EVIDENCE. The verification gate did
its job. It found that 007–010 had INSERTED the section at §06 and pushed
Roads to §07, while 001–004/006 AND the template still ran the original
18-section shape. The premise behind APPEND was that renumbering breaks
anchors; a corpus scan of all 112 repo files returned ZERO external anchor
links and ZERO textual §-references into 001–006, and R30 carries no
profile links at all. So renumbering broke nothing, while appending would
have left identical content at §06 in four profiles and §19 in five —
precisely the cross-document divergence the content audit diagnosed as the
library's central weakness. Recorded because the lesson generalises: a
ratification made on an assumption is provisional until the assumption is
measured, and the gate that measures it must run before the spec, not after.

RA-1 UNDATED (owner decision, 22 Jul 2026). The former pre-departure
deadline on the loaded weighbridge is WITHDRAWN. It happens when
convenient. NEVER RE-ATTACH A DATE — this is a standing instruction, not a
one-off deferral. Two locations amended in this file (the VEHICLE weights
bullet and the RA-1 entry in the READINESS ACTIONS block); OI-1 in the Open

CONSEQUENCES RECORDED, not glossed: (1) the deadline was not arbitrary —
it made the Karoo loop double as a loaded shakedown at known weight;
undated, the loop still runs, but its R23 §7 consumption actuals come from
a vehicle of unmeasured mass, which weakens them as EX-8 calibration data.
Logged as a known limitation of that data set, NOT a blocker. (2) The real
risk is not the delay but the hardening: three kerb/payload estimate pairs
circulate, and an indefinite delay is exactly the condition under which a
provisional figure becomes authoritative through repetition. Both amended
locations therefore carry an explicit PROVISIONAL clause, and any future
session that finds a kerb or payload figure presented as settled must
re-flag it rather than propagate it.

F71 REFRAMED (why it was pulled forward). Set the six profiles lacking §06
against Option A: Angola, Namibia, Botswana, Zambia, Kalahari and Tanzania
cover the shakedown leg, the dry-season run up the Zambezi and the
wet-season base — roughly months 1–12. The four that DO carry §06 cover
Year 2 and the southbound return. Parts and serviceability coverage is
therefore strongest where the crew will be most practised and absent where
they will be greenest and furthest from any workshop they have used. That
is a risk-weighting error, not a documentation gap. Second reason: the EX-8
R3 Expedition Consumables Ledger keys its carry-versus-source-en-route
logic to the profile serviceability data, so six missing sections block
EX-8's principal deliverable for the first year of the route.

copy of LC76_Profile_TEMPLATE.html runs EIGHTEEN sections with §06 = Roads;
profiles 007–010 apparently use §06 = Parts & Serviceability. Either the

the change spec. This determines append-versus-renumber definitively and
settles whether the template itself needs correcting so the gap cannot
recur. Project knowledge lags GitHub — F17 in the June audit was precisely
this class of error.

OPEN GATE for Session 1 (default recorded, owner to confirm or collapse):
profiles already carry a content-currency badge and the standing convention
is not to touch it. The F72 marker is a different instrument — the badge is
DOCUMENT-level provenance (when the profile was compiled), the marker is
DATA-CLASS-level (how old is this fuel price). DEFAULT ADOPTED: keep both,
leave the badge untouched, place the marker at the head of each perishable
section, inheriting the R35 §8 V/M/S evidence-grade convention with
row-level grades only where a wrong number costs money or safety (fees,
fuel prices, security posture).

STANDING ADDITIONS TO THE WORKING CONVENTIONS from this programme
(promoted into WORKING CONVENTIONS on 22 Jul 2026):
(a) any specific numeric specification introduced into a library
document must be traceable to Facts, the owning R-doc, or a manual —
plausible numbers read as authority and are the most dangerous thing to
add to a field reference (F65); (b) self-audit of prior-session edits is
a standing step in every audit session; (c) audit/governance HTML files
are index-linked but never precached and never bump CACHE; (d) a
candidate finding is verified before it is written down — a false
finding costs the owner's trust in the whole register.

── B000 · START · sha256 c0c843fa6091afde · ▼▼▼ START — PROJECT INSTRUCTIONS ▼▼▼ This project builds and
▼▼▼ START — PROJECT INSTRUCTIONS ▼▼▼

This project builds and maintains the LC76 Overland Reference Library for Neil
& Mary-Ann's 2009 Toyota Land Cruiser 76 Series SW (HZJ76R-RKMRS), based in
South Africa. The vehicle is used for serious overlanding across Southern and
East Africa, with a planned two-year, 60,000+ km expedition in 2028–2030 (distance
figure confirmed authoritative 9 Jul 2026 — supersedes the ~40,000 km working
figure). En-route service policy (owner, 9 Jul 2026): follow the service
schedule at Toyota dealers where the route allows; oil changes are never
deferred — every en-route oil change is also an RA-2 oil-analysis sampling point.

INDEX LAYOUT (restructured 21 Jul 2026 — situation-based): index.html is
organised by reader situation, urgency-descending, EIGHT sections:
1 Emergency & Safety · 2 Find Anything (R30) · 3 Field Tools (R1 +
calculators) · 4 Vehicle (four h3 sub-groups: Engine & Mechanical /
Drivetrain & Tyres / Battery & Electrical / Spares & Service Record) ·
5 The Expedition · 6 Country Profiles & Trips · 7 Philosophy & Ethos ·
8 Governance. Principles: crisis content first (stress-state design);

── B042 · DESIGN SYSTEM (mandatory · sha256 7581177d0afcc2ab · Masthead standard (DG v1.6, section S10) — six slots, fixed 
- Masthead standard (DG v1.6, section S10) — six slots, fixed order:
  back-link (⌂ Index) · eyebrow (Collection · ID) · title (Title Case) ·
  one-line subtitle (≤~12 words) · invariant identity line · Last-updated date.
  Identity line, identical on every file: "2009 LC76 SW · 1HZ 4.2L Diesel ·
  low-boost turbo (0.6 bar) · intercooler". NEVER in a masthead: odometer, GVM,
  per-document component specs, the EX-programme tag, or a vehicle code in the
  eyebrow. Closed collection vocabulary (six): Reference · Country Profile ·
  Terrain Profile · Field Tool · Philosophy · Itinerary. Profiles carry ONE
  extra element — native official name as a de-emphasised line under the title;
  their entry/visa/plating strip moves to the body. Exempt: calculators (no
  back-link/identity/date, eyebrow "Field Tool") and governance/infrastructure
  (index.html, Design Guidelines, Profile Template, audit HTML). Date form:
  "Last updated: D Month YYYY" (lowercase, full month, no zero-pad).
  R8-supplement eyebrow form: "Reference · R8 Supplement".
  PHILOSOPHY — ONE EXTRA ELEMENT (DG v1.9, adopted 29 Jul 2026). The four
  philosophy documents carry an editorial gloss as a de-emphasised italic
  .std-local line placed BELOW THE SUBTITLE and above the identity line —
  deliberately NOT the profile placement (profiles put .std-local directly
  under the title), because the philosophy .std-sub is a small mono amber
  line, typographically the same instrument as the profile .std-local, and
  the profile placement stacks two near-identical lines. Slots 5 and 6 are
  unaffected. The philosophy batch of the S10 migration completed 29 Jul
  2026; the itinerary half ran 28 Jul.

── B044 · DESIGN SYSTEM (mandatory · sha256 95f0c5f75d79bad7 · TOC IS ONE ROW AT EVERY WIDTH (mandatory, DG v1.8, adopted 2
- TOC IS ONE ROW AT EVERY WIDTH (mandatory, DG v1.8, adopted 29 Jul 2026).
  The single-row horizontal scroller lives in the BASE rule, never in a
  max-width media query: flex-wrap:nowrap; overflow-x:auto;
  overscroll-behavior-x:contain; -webkit-overflow-scrolling:touch;
  scrollbar-width:none — plus .toc-bar::-webkit-scrollbar{display:none} at
  base. The child selector needs white-space:nowrap ONLY; no flex
  declarations. THE SCROLLBAR STAYS HIDDEN on desktop too (owner ruling,
  29 Jul) — do NOT add a @media (pointer:fine) scrollbar to individual
  files. REASONING: the TOC is inside the sticky unit, so a wrapped row
  costs viewport on every screen of the document, permanently. A TOC that
  wraps is LATENT until someone adds a section — the Karoo file was fine
  at 17 buttons and became a three-row header at 19. If a row gets
  tediously long, SHORTEN THE LABELS (the section h2 carries the full
  title), do not widen the bar.

── B047 · DESIGN SYSTEM (mandatory · sha256 0cb985b49a5f2085 · TOC INDEX CONTRACT (mandatory, adopted 22 Jul 2026 from OI-3
- TOC INDEX CONTRACT (mandatory, adopted 22 Jul 2026 from OI-38;
  CORRECTED the same day after the first version of this rule caused three
  regressions). THE CONTRACT HAS THREE PARTS, NOT TWO: the sectionIds
  array, the TOC buttons, AND THE TOGGLE EXPRESSION that compares them.
  Read the toggle in the file before judging the pairing — do not assume it.
  Under toggle i===idx the array must mirror the TOC buttons exactly (same
  entries, same order, same length, including any alerts and changelog
  entries). Under toggle (i-1)===idx the array must be the TOC list MINUS
  its leading button, and an array that looks "short" is correct. Both
  conventions existed in this library and a rule written from one of them
  broke three files built on the other. STANDARDISED 22 Jul 2026: all ten
  profiles and the template now use i===idx with a 1:1 array, and any new
  profile inherits that from the template. If a future file is found on the
  offset convention, normalise it rather than adjusting the array. A mismatch does not throw; it silently highlights
  the wrong button, so it will never be caught by node --check or a depth
  walk. Two mirror-image failures were found in the profile family: an
  'alerts' id in the array with no button (003), and an "! Alerts" button
  absent from the array (006 and the TEMPLATE). ADD TO THE STANDARD
  VALIDATION SET: assert len(sectionIds) == len(tocLinks), assert every
  sectionIds entry resolves to a real element id, and verify the pairing in
  order. Whenever a section is added or removed, both lists change together.
  HOW TO EXTRACT THE TOC SIDE (learned the hard way, 22 Jul 2026 — two
  extractor versions gave false readings before the third was trusted):
  compare sectionIds against the getElementById TARGETS taken from every
  element carrying class="toc-link". Do NOT compare against button LABELS;
  do NOT assume the element is an <a>, because 009 Zimbabwe uses
  <span class="toc-link" onclick=...> and read as zero buttons; and do NOT
  anchor the pattern on attribute ORDER, because a class-first regex misses
  every file where class is the last attribute and will report most of the
  family as broken. The safe form is: find all tags matching
  <[a-zA-Z]+[^>]*class="toc-link"[^>]*>, then pull getElementById('X') from
  each tag. Both false readings were caught before being reported — working
  convention (d) doing exactly what it exists for.

── B050 · DESIGN SYSTEM (mandatory · sha256 85c7c2f4ec636275 · Print CSS: Design Guidelines Section 16 print standard — 7mm
- Print CSS: Design Guidelines Section 16 print standard — 7mm page margins,
  7.5pt body, no forced page breaks by default, break-inside:avoid on small
  elements only, break-inside:auto + thead repeat on large tables (15+ rows).
  ** THE 7.5pt BODY RULE HAS NEVER TAKEN EFFECT ANYWHERE — see OI-60. Sizes in
  this library are declared in rem, which resolves against the ROOT element;
  the print blocks set BODY. Diagnostic 29 Jul 2026: zero of 71 files set the
  root size in print, 1,547 rem declarations sit in base CSS, and printed pages
  have been coming out at screen size throughout. The remedy is a single root
  declaration inside @media print plus rem heading overrides that COMPRESS the
  hierarchy — prototyped on LC76_How_To_Use_This_Library.html (knob 11.6px =
  8pt body, owner-ruled off a real printout after 12pt was trialled and
  rejected). DG §16 must be corrected BEFORE any propagation; until it is,
  §16 specifies a form that cannot work. DO NOT record a file as §16-compliant
  on the strength of the rule being present. **
  Firefox: redefine CSS custom properties as hex with !important inside
  @media print — var(--token) does not resolve reliably in Firefox print.
