── B065 · WORKING CONVENTIONS · sha256 d8aaa43a5e4a2568 · Ownership-first propagation (adopted 22 Jul 2026, from the c
- Ownership-first propagation (adopted 22 Jul 2026, from the content-audit
  seam pattern — the sharpest of the three structural findings). Before
  editing any topic, look it up in R30 and identify the OWNING document.
  Edit the owner FIRST, then every document that delegates to it, in the
  SAME session — never the reverse. R42-then-R10 is the failure mode that
  produced F38 (R42 corrected while R10 §3, which R42 delegates to, still
  carried superseded fire doctrine); R10-then-R42 would not have produced
  it. SESSION-CLOSE COMPLETENESS ASSERTION: for each topic touched, list
  the documents that delegate to it and assert each was visited, or
  explicitly deferred to a numbered open item — same control class as the
  COUNTIF baseline on the working papers. An R30 OWNER-flag column, which
  would convert this discipline from an act of vigilance into a lookup, is
  approved in principle and DEFERRED until after the Karoo loop.

── B067 · WORKING CONVENTIONS · sha256 04baccd9229ca9af · Status-bearing content carries provenance (standing, adopted
- Status-bearing content carries provenance (standing, adopted 29 Jul 2026,
  from OI-67). Any array, table or list in this library that asserts a live
  STATUS — outstanding actions, monitors, flags, "no record" claims — must
  carry a source field naming where each entry came from (manual, service
  history, owning R-doc, workshop advisory). A list without provenance cannot
  be audited, therefore is not maintained, therefore drifts — the Service
  Checklist's OUTSTANDING array carried two items that had been settled weeks
  earlier, on a document with a print-and-take-to-the-workshop function. The
  convention already existed one array away in the same file. COROLLARY FOR
  RECONCILIATION: a completeness gate must sweep for status-bearing content
  WHEREVER IT LIVES, not only in instruments that look like registers. The
  29 Jul gate checked spreadsheets and audit reports, declared the register
  substantially complete, and missed both R8 and its supplement.

── B070 · WORKING CONVENTIONS · sha256 bef530943a6e6b34 · Changelog discipline (owner ruling, 22 Jul 2026). (a) EVERY 
- Changelog discipline (owner ruling, 22 Jul 2026). (a) EVERY masthead
  "Last updated" bump carries a matching changelog entry — the masthead is
  the claim, the changelog is the evidence, and a claim without evidence is
  the defect class the content audit spent five sessions removing. This
  applies to silent fixes too: "TOC highlighting corrected, no content
  changed" is a valid entry. EXEMPTION, ADOPTED 29 JUL 2026 (owner ruling):
  rule (a) does NOT apply to non-profile R-docs, which carry no changelog
  section. The EVIDENCE INSTRUMENT FOR THAT CLASS IS NAMED INSTEAD —
  LC76_Session_History.txt and LC76_Library_Register.txt, which record what
  changed, when and why, per session. The principle is unchanged: a masthead
  claim still requires evidence; the evidence lives centrally rather than in
  the file. THE ALTERNATIVE WAS MEASURED AND REJECTED: adding changelog
  sections to ~40 R-docs would touch the three-part TOC index contract in
  every one of them — the structure that produced three regressions on
  22 Jul — and the back-catalogue could not be populated without
  reconstructing history, which doctrine forbids; each file would have
  gained a changelog whose only entry recorded its own creation. Profiles
  keep rule (a) because they are perishable, country-specific and read
  standalone by someone deciding whether a fee is current. THIS IS AN
  EXEMPTION, NOT AN OVERSIGHT — recorded so a later session does not "fix"
  it. (b) Every profile carries a changelog.
  (c) Changelog dates are ISO (2026-07-22) — sortable and unambiguous.
  (d) The content-currency badge ("Compiled 7 Jul 2026") is NOT a changelog
  date and is never normalised or bumped with these edits — it is
  document-level provenance and has its own convention.
  STRUCTURAL DIVERGENCE, UNRESOLVED (OI-39): 001–008 carry the changelog as
  an UNNUMBERED section (id="changelog") with .changelog-entry grid rows;
  009 and 010 carry it as the FINAL NUMBERED SECTION (§19 / §20) as a
  revision table. Both are valid and both now satisfy (a)–(c). Harmonising

── B093 · WORKING CONVENTIONS · sha256 613ef97274340185 · The register is a database with a schema (standing, adopted 
- The register is a database with a schema (standing, adopted 29 Jul
  2026). Every open entry carries, in fixed order: the technical title;
  a «WHAT:» line in plain English with no codes; OWNER · GATE · SOURCE;
  and a STATUS line standing alone. The WHAT line is not decoration —

  to everyone else, INCLUDING THE SECOND CREW MEMBER ON WHOM RA-8 SOLO
  CAPABILITY DEPENDS. A SCHEMA IS ONLY REAL IF SOMETHING REJECTS
  VIOLATIONS: a session-close validator that parses every entry and
  fails loudly on non-conformance is a Session 5 deliverable, and until
  it exists the schema is a wish, not a control.

── B096 · WORKING CONVENTIONS · sha256 587fc92cfd393892 · Governance files delivered whole, never as patches (standing
- Governance files delivered whole, never as patches (standing, 26 Jul
  2026; HARDENED 30 Jul 2026 after a repeated failure). Governance .txt files
  (PI, OIR, Facts, Register) are delivered as COMPLETE, WHOLE FILES — every
  single time, no exceptions. THE OWNER DOES NOT HAND-APPLY ANYTHING. Claude
  MUST NEVER: produce a delta / diff / patch file; produce a partial file with
  a "continue in the next session" stop-note; ask the owner to paste, splice,
  merge, or insert snippets "here and there"; or hand back anything the owner
  must assemble himself. The owner's workflow is RECEIVE-COMPLETE-FILE →
  UPLOAD/COMMIT IT. A patch file is also an orphan of the OI-32/OI-42 class —
  it updates neither the GitHub file nor the PI field. THE OBJECTION THAT
  STOPPED THIS BEFORE — "transcribing 150 KB from context risks silent drift" —
  IS NOT A LICENCE TO DELIVER A PARTIAL FILE. It is a reason to BUILD THE WHOLE
  FILE THE SAFE WAY: assemble it in parts on disk (create_file per part),
  concatenate with bash, then RECONCILE programmatically before presenting —
  assert the structural invariants (one masthead date line, exactly one START
  marker line and one END marker line, no stop-note fragments, clean seams, the
  cross-file next-free == OIR max + 1). That is the same anchored-str.replace +
  before/after reconciliation discipline used on every other governance file;
  it removes the drift risk WITHOUT ever shipping a partial. When the PI lives
  on disk (GitHub / a gist / an upload), edit it in place with count==1
  str.replace asserts; when it lives only in context, rebuild-and-reconcile as
  above. Either way the deliverable is ONE COMPLETE FILE.

── B101 · WORKING CONVENTIONS · sha256 999cc90230119e14 · Content-currency is gated, not remembered (standing, adopted
- Content-currency is gated, not remembered (standing, adopted 22 Sep 2026).
  The fact gate catches NUMERIC drift; depth-walks catch STRUCTURE; NEITHER can
  see stale PROSE — a part swapped on the vehicle (Racor->Flashlube) or a status
  that turned (planned->fitted). The rejecter for that class is
  lc76_currency_gate.py, driven by lc76_supersessions.txt: every real-world change
  that retires a term in the corpus is ONE old->new row in the log, and ADDING that
  row is what ARMS the gate against the old term — so a new change cannot slip past
  without arming the check. This is the answer to "a hardcoded term-list fails the
  moment something new changes": the list is DATA in the log, not code in a script,
  and the log row is the same line you write to record the change. WORKFLOW: on any
  real-world entity/status change, add the supersession row FIRST, then fix the
  prose, then run the gate to zero fail-tier hits (warn-tier is surfaced for review
  — historical ledgers, comparison tables and correction notes are legitimate).
  HONEST LIMIT: it catches SUPERSESSIONS that go through the log, not brand-new
  fabrications; log-first is the discipline. It is the enforcement arm of the
  Controlled Duplication Register for named-entity/status currency, and runs at
  session close beside the fact gate. THE LESSON THAT FORCED IT: the tyre and
  Flashlube sweeps used FILE LISTS not a corpus-wide grep (the G4 failure mode) and
  the fact gate could not see the stale names, so "Racor"/"planned"/a layout bug
  survived every automated check until the owner caught them. A gate the owner has
  to run in his own head is not a gate.

── B102 · WORKING CONVENTIONS · sha256 898f9cca9859fef8 · Roll-forward reconciliation on governance files (standing, a
- Roll-forward reconciliation on governance files (standing, adopted
  24 Jul 2026). The validation pipeline was built from HTML defects and is
  made of HTML instruments; the governance .txt files — the Open Items
  Register, Facts, the Library Register, this file — are spliced by the
  same str.replace technique and had NO structural gate at all. A
  duplicated "CLOSED LOG" header survived two commits because depth walks
  and node --check cannot see it. The fix is deliberately NOT a checklist:
  a checklist only catches the failure modes someone anticipated, and
  nobody anticipated that one. Instead, reconcile the file the way a
  ledger is reconciled — opening balance, declared movements, closing
  balance, and anything unexplained is a finding.
  METHOD: before editing, snapshot the file's structural invariants; state
  the intended delta; recompute after; assert the difference equals
  exactly what was declared. For the OIR the invariants are the multiset
  of section headers, the set of OI IDs with the section each occupies,
  the count of structural headers, and the closed-log entry count. For
  Facts: section numbers unique and sequential, plus the authoritative
  figures (odometer, GVM, kerb, payload) extracted before and after — an
  UNDECLARED change to a payload figure is exactly what should trip an
  alarm while those figures remain PROVISIONAL.
  CROSS-FILE CHECK, run at session close: assert this file's "next free"
  number equals the register's maximum OI plus one. MEASURE ITEM
  DEFINITIONS, NOT MENTIONS — match line-start "OI-nn" followed by a
  title, because closed-log prose routinely names numbers ("next free is
  now OI-46") and a naive scan reads those as items and reports a false
  failure. This probe did exactly that on its first run, 24 Jul 2026, and
  the file was correct. Same class as the id="changelog" probe error at
  OI-38: when a probe disagrees with the file, suspect the probe first. Two sessions both
  believing the same number is free is a collision no single-file gate can
  see.
  SCOPE THE PI SIDE TOO (added 28 Jul 2026, from a probe error made while
  building the status gate below). The authoritative "next free" is the one
  in the CURRENT session header block. This file ALSO carries historical
  "next free OI-nn" strings inside every archived session block, so a probe
  taking the LAST match in the file reads an old number and reports a false
  mismatch — it read OI-48 against a correct OI-54. Scope the extraction to
  the header block, not the whole file. Third probe error in one session,
  all three caught before being reported as file defects.
  SECOND CROSS-FILE CHECK — STATUS, NOT JUST NUMBERING (standing, adopted
  28 Jul 2026, from a live defect). The check above compares NUMBERING
  only. It cannot see a narrative in THIS file asserting an item is OPEN
  when the register records it CLOSED, and that gap shipped: the
  nineteenth-session block said OI-32/OI-42 "stay OPEN until the owner
  confirms the files are gone" when both had been closed the same day with
  HTTP 404 verification on every file. The claim survived a session and was
  then reported to the owner as live work outstanding. AT SESSION CLOSE:
  extract every OI-nn in this file's HEADER NARRATIVE carrying an ATTACHED
  status claim, and assert none appears in the register's closed log.
  WRITE THE PROBE TIGHT OR IT WILL CRY WOLF. A proximity window — "OI-nn
  within N characters of OPEN or pending" — over-reported by 100% on its
  first run, matching "OI-50 OPENED & CLOSED" and an unrelated "PENDING"
  about an index.html card two sentences away. The status claim must be
  ATTACHED to the reference — OI-nn[/OI-mm] (stays|remains|is|are)
  (OPEN|pending|unresolved) — not merely near it. Same lesson as the
  id="changelog" and next-free probes: when a probe disagrees with the
  file, suspect the probe first.
  AND NOTE WHAT CAUSED THE DIVERGENCE. The register is authoritative for
  what is OPEN; this file's narrative records WHAT HAPPENED. Where they
  disagree on STATUS, THE REGISTER WINS. The divergence exists because
  open-item DETAIL was duplicated into the narrative — which is exactly
  what the standing convention forbids: keep a POINTER here, not the
  detail. A duplicated fact is a seam that reports no error.
  LIMITATION, STATED SO THE GATE DOES NOT CREATE FALSE COMFORT: this
  catches SHAPE, NOT TRUTH. A well-formed entry containing an invented
  number passes every one of these assertions. The character-count error
  of 24 Jul was caught by working convention (d), verify-before-you-report,
  and not by any structural gate. They are different controls doing
  different jobs and neither substitutes for the other.

── B103 · WORKING CONVENTIONS · sha256 73e53a41b668616e · Idempotence, not just anchors (standing, adopted 24 Jul 2026
- Idempotence, not just anchors (standing, adopted 24 Jul 2026, from a live
  defect). `assert count == 1` proves the ANCHOR is unique; it proves
  nothing about whether the edit has already been applied. When a
  replacement CONTAINS its own anchor — the common case for "keep the old
  block and add a new one after it" — the guard passes on every re-run and
  silently duplicates the insertion. This happened on R32 on 24 Jul: a
  script re-run after two unrelated anchor failures applied the same block
  twice. Any script that might be re-run must ALSO assert that a
  distinctive string from the NEW content is ABSENT before writing, and a
  post-edit count of that string should be 1. Recovery is a SHA-pinned
  re-fetch and a single clean application.

── B104 · WORKING CONVENTIONS · sha256 6bb0205b7198f018 · Encode before you truncate (standing, adopted 24 Jul 2026, f
- Encode before you truncate (standing, adopted 24 Jul 2026, from a live
  defect). Never write an edited file with open(path,'w') — that truncates
  the target the instant it is called, so any later failure (a bad encode, a
  lone surrogate in an emoji, an exception mid-write) destroys the file
  before a single validation gate has run. Encode the full string to bytes
  FIRST, confirm that succeeded, then write binary. The validation pipeline
  runs after the write and therefore cannot protect against this class at
  all; only the write pattern can. Recovery is a single SHA-pinned fetch,
  which is one more reason pinning is not optional.

── B111 · WORKING CONVENTIONS · sha256 d42d65493987cffe · Project-knowledge currency (standing, adopted 27 Jul 2026, f
- Project-knowledge currency (standing, adopted 27 Jul 2026, from OI-49).
  Project knowledge carries NO duplicate of a governance file that lives on
  GitHub. Facts, the Library Register and the Project Instructions were all
  found there five to six weeks stale, the PI fossil at 7% the size of the
  real file, and were removed. If such a copy is ever added back, it is
  refreshed in the SAME session as the GitHub edit or it is deleted — never
  left to age. THE REASONING IS THE GENERAL ONE THIS LIBRARY KEEPS
  RELEARNING: a stale duplicate is a seam that reports no error. Absence is
  safer than staleness, because absence forces the correct behaviour (fetch
  live, SHA-pinned) while a wrong copy invites the incorrect one. NOTE THE
  DISTINCTION that makes this safe: the Project Instructions FIELD is a
  different mechanism from a project-knowledge FILE — the field is injected
  automatically into every session and is what actually governs behaviour,
  so it must be kept current; the file was pure duplication and is gone.

── B112 · WORKING CONVENTIONS · sha256 486b443d6e08581d · Archives are not reconstructed, and a redacted archive says 
- Archives are not reconstructed, and a redacted archive says so
  (standing; promoted 29 Jul 2026 from the twentieth/nineteenth-session
  narratives ahead of their archiving). The Open Items Register and
  LC76_Session_History.txt are ARCHIVES. Their narrative is never altered,
  removed or rewritten after the fact — a correction is an ANNOTATION
  against the original sentence, not a replacement of it, because a
  reconstructed record reads as contemporaneous when it is not (the F65
  class applied to governance). TWO COROLLARIES. (i) Withdrawing a VALUE is
  a different act from altering a RECORD, but a reader who finds a pointer
  where a number used to be must be told why: an archive that has been
  redacted carries a DATED REDACTION NOTICE naming the class of data
  withdrawn, stating that no narrative was altered, and noting that prior
  commits still hold the originals. Otherwise the redaction itself becomes
  the invisible seam. (ii) A missing session is recorded as a LABELLED,
  DATED GAP and sessions are NEVER RENUMBERED to close it — renumbering
  would silently re-point every existing cross-reference to the wrong
  ordinal.

── B117 · WORKING CONVENTIONS · sha256 442973fa9ea0f4eb · Publish trips retrospectively (standing, adopted 28 Jul 2026
- Publish trips retrospectively (standing, adopted 28 Jul 2026, Gate C of
  the Karoo date-exposure pass). A trip itinerary published BEFORE or
  DURING the trip carries RELATIVE DAY NUMBERING (Day 1, Day 2 …) or a
  season ("winter 2026"); exact calendar dates are held in the private
  annex and go into the document only once the crew is home. THE REASONING:
  a dated day-by-day schedule, set against a named crew, a distinctive
  vehicle and a known region, publishes precisely which nights a South
  African property stands empty and for how long. Note the scope — this is
  NOT a two-date problem. The Karoo itinerary carried 22 distinct date
  tokens across 55 occurrences; generalising only departure and return
  would have left every intermediate leg dated. Anyone scoping this class
  of edit as a two-string change has mis-scoped it.
  CRITICALLY, THIS IS A TEMPORARY SUPPRESSION WITH A DEFINED RESTORATION
  POINT, not a permanent redaction of the RC1 class. Dates are dangerous
  BEFORE the fact and harmless AFTER it, and afterwards they carry real
  documentary value — they are the record of a real trip and the basis of
  the R23 §7 post-trip review. Annex sections holding trip dates therefore
  carry a RESTORE-AFTER note, which is the OPPOSITE instruction from the
  permanent §1/§2/§3 and MUST NOT be copied from them by pattern. The
  restoration moment is the post-trip review session itself.

- Personal-data discipline (adopted 20 Jul 2026; EXTENDED AND PARTLY
  SUPERSEDED 28 Jul 2026 by the RC1 / name reduction pass). SA ID numbers

  repo is public. Library documents carry blank fill-in lines; completed

  (VIN, engine, registration) are already public-facing and may appear."
  That is now SPLIT, because it was being read to cover the registration
  CERTIFICATE as well as the vehicle:
  · STILL PUBLIC — VIN, engine serial, chassis code, the CURRENT
    registration plate, and the tare figure. Each is stamped on the

    description, first-licensed date, PREVIOUS licence number, and

    exist only on a document a stranger should not hold; together with
    the registered owner's full legal name they corroborate a false
    provenance story.

    files. FIRST NAMES are retained deliberately throughout the library
    — a first name is not a join key, and removing ~280 instances would

  and a dated absence window they form a usable package for registration

  could a stranger do with it that he could not do without it.

  was NOT rewritten (owner ruling, 28 Jul 2026). A filter-repo rewrite
  would rewrite every SHA and break the SHA-pinning convention the whole
  working method rests on, for material that is either self-expiring or
  visible on the vehicle. Redaction is forward-looking only.

── B132 · SOURCE OF TRUTH / PROJEC · sha256 638a4599f5cb42fb · SOURCE OF TRUTH / PROJECT KNOWLEDGE POLICY
SOURCE OF TRUTH / PROJECT KNOWLEDGE POLICY

── B133 · SOURCE OF TRUTH / PROJEC · sha256 d8a656f684560f7b · Source of truth is SPLIT INTO TWO DOMAINS (SSOT programme, O
- Source of truth is SPLIT INTO TWO DOMAINS (SSOT programme, OI-107, adopted
  18 Sep 2026 — supersedes the blanket rule for the fact domain only). PROSE
  domain: GitHub / the HTML document remains the single source of truth for a
  document's authored prose. FACT domain: LC76_Facts.txt is the single source
  of truth for FACTS — the fact-values shown in HTML (and in Facts’ own
  readable lines) are DOWNSTREAM RELEASED COPIES, never hand-edited. A fact is
  edited in the store and released to the documents by lc76_fact_release.py;
  the gate (lc76_fact_gate.py) FAILS on any tagged slot that disagrees with the
  store, and the fact-store validator (LC76_Fact_Validator.py) rejects a
  malformed store. WORKFLOW: edit the fact in LC76_Facts.txt -> run
  lc76_fact_release.py -> gate passes (or lists every drift) -> commit; a
  monthly odometer reading is one store line + release, never a library-wide
  hand-sweep. Governing architecture: LC76_Single_Source_of_Truth_Design.txt.
  THE FACT STORE IS A MAINTENANCE-MONITORING INSTRUMENT (G1, 19 Sep 2026), not
  only a drift-preventer: a service-interval fact whose km-since meets or exceeds
  its interval is a SAFETY ALERT, and the odometer reading is the trigger that
  surfaces overruns — a primary purpose of the programme (see the OVERRUN
  convention in WORKING CONVENTIONS). Directly serves the "do not break down
  because something was missed" goal.
  Project
  knowledge holds only the lightweight authoritative texts. Never edit a
  document against a project-knowledge copy — always fetch live, SHA-PINNED:
  take the HEAD SHA from the repo Atom feed
  (github.com/Overlandslabs/Consumption/commits/main.atom — the first
  /commit/<40-hex>), then fetch
  raw.githubusercontent.com/Overlandslabs/Consumption/<SHA>/<filename>.
